# Siva Purana. Chapter 1

## Section 1 - Vidyeśvara-saṃhitā

## Chapter 1 - The Doubt of the Sages

## \|\| 1.1.1-2 \|\|

*ādyantamaṃgalamajātasamānabhāvamāryaṃ tamīśamajarāmaramātmadevam \|*  
*paṃcānanaṃ prabalapaṃcavinodaśīlaṃ saṃbhāvaye manasiśaṃkaramambikeśam \|*  
*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*dharmakṣetre mahākṣetre gaṃgākālindisaṃgame \|*  
*prayāge parame puṇye brahmalokasya vartmani \|\| 1 \|\|*

*munayaḥ śaṃsitātmānassatyavrataparāyaṇāḥ \|*  
*mahaujaso mahābhāgā mahāsatraṃ vitenire \|\| 2 \|\|*

(Benedictory Prayer)

I meditate on Śiva, the lord of Ambikā (Pārvatī), auspicious from the beginning to the end, having no parallel, the noble lord, the unaging and the undying, the lord of Ātmans, the five-faced (see appendix on the five faces of Śiva) and the dispeller of the five powerful sins.

Vyāsa said:—

\[1\]

1-2. Sages of edified souls, engaged in truthful rites, powerful and blessed, performed a great sacrifice at the confluence of Gaṅgā and Kālindī (Yamunā) in the most sacred city of Prayāga,\[2\] a great holy centre, the path that leads to Brahmaloka.

## \|\| 1.1.3 \|\|

*tatra satraṃ samākarṇya vyāsaśiṣyo mahāmuniḥ \|*  
*ājagāma munīndra ṣṭuṃ sūtaḥ paurāṇikottamaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. On hearing that a sacrifice was being performed there, the disciple of Vyāsa, the great sage Sūta, an excellent scholar in the Purāṇas, arrived there to see the sages.

## \|\| 1.1.4 \|\|

*taṃ dṛṣṭvā sūtamāyāṃtaṃ harṣitā munayastadā \|*  
*cetasā suprasannena pūjāṃ cakruryathāvidhi \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. The sages were delighted on seeing him and received him with due hospitality and adoration.

## \|\| 1.1.5 \|\|

*tato vinayasaṃyuktā procuḥ sāṃjalayaśca te \|*  
*suprasannā mahātmānaḥ stutiṃ kṛtvāyathāvidhi \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. The due adoration being completed, the noble sages, being highly pleased, addressed him in all humility with their palms joined in reverence.

## \|\| 1.1.6 \|\|

*romaharṣaṇa sarvajña bhavānvai bhāgyagauravāt \|*  
*purāṇavidyāmakhilāṃ vyāsātpratyarthamīyivān \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. O Romaharṣaṇa, the omniscient, by thy weighty fortune, the entire Purāṇic lore, pregnant in its meaningful content, has been secured by thee from Vyāsa.

## \|\| 1.1.7 \|\|

*tasmādāścaryyabhūtānāṃ kathānāṃ tvaṃ hi bhājanam \|*  
*ratnānāmurusārāṇāṃ ratnākara ivārṇavaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Hence thou art the receptacle of wonder-inspiring stories, even as the vast ocean is the storehouse of gems of great worth.

## \|\| 1.1.8 \|\|

*yacca bhūtaṃ ca bhavyaṃ ca yaccānyadvastu vartate \|*  
*na tvayā'viditaṃ kiṃcittriṣu lokeṣu vidyate \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. There is nothing in the three worlds that is not known to thee, of the past, present and the future.

## \|\| 1.1.9 \|\|

*tvaṃ maddiṣṭavaśādasya darśanārthamihāgataḥ \|*  
*kurvankimapi naḥ śreyo na vṛthā gaṃtumarhasi \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. It is our great fortune that thou thyself hast come to pay a visit to us. Hence it is not proper on thy part to return without doing us a favour.

## \|\| 1.1.10 \|\|

*tattvaṃ śrutaṃ sma naḥ sarvaṃ pūrvameva śubhāśubham \|*  
*na tṛptimadhigacchāmaḥ śravaṇecchā muhurmuhuḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. It is true that we have already listened to the explanation of the auspicious and the inauspicious. But we are not content. We yearn to hear more and more.

## \|\| 1.1.11 \|\|

*idānīmekamevāsti śrotavyaṃ sūta sanmate \|*  
*tadra hasyamapi brūhi yadi te'nugraho bhavet \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Now, O Sūta of good mentality, we have only one point to be clarified. If thou dost desire to bless us, please explain the same, though it be the secret of secrets.

## \|\| 1.1.12 \|\|

*prāpte kaliyuge ghore narāḥ puṇyavivarjitāḥ \|*  
*durācāraratāḥ sarve satyavārtāparāṅmukhāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. At the advent of the terrible age of Kali men have become devoid of merits. They are engaged in evil ways of life. They have turned their faces from truthful avocations.

## \|\| 1.1.13 \|\|

*parāpavādaniratāḥ paradra vyābhilāṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*parastrīsaktamanasaḥ parahiṃsāparāyaṇāḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. They are engaged in caluminating others. They covet other men’s wealth. Their attention is diverted to other men’s wives. Injuring others has become their chief aim.

## \|\| 1.1.14 \|\|

*dehātmadṛṣṭayā mūḍhā nāstikāḥ paśubuddhayaḥ \|*  
*mātṛpitṛkṛtadveṣāḥ strīdevāḥ kāmakiṃkarāḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. They view the physical body as the soul, deluded as they are; they are atheists of mere brutish sense; they hate their parents; their wives are goddesses unto them; they are slaves to lust.

## \|\| 1.1.15 \|\|

*viprā lobhagrahagrastā vedavikrayajīvinaḥ \|*  
*dhanārjanārthamabhyastavidyā madavimohitāḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Brahmins are in the clutches of greed, they sell Vedas for livelihood; they acquire learning as a means of earning money; they are deluded by their false pride.

## \|\| 1.1.16 \|\|

*tyaktasvajātikarmāṇaḥ prāyaśaḥparavaṃcakāḥ \|*  
*trikālasaṃdhyayā hīnā brahmabodhavivarjitāḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. They have forsaken the duties of their own castes; they have almost become swindlers of others; they do not offer Sandhyā prayers thrice a day; they are deprived of Vedic enlightenment.

## \|\| 1.1.17 \|\|

*adayāḥ paṃḍitaṃ manyāssvācāravratalopakāḥ \|*  
*kṛṣyudyamaratāḥ krūrasvabhāvā malināśayāḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. They are ruthless; they make much of their little knowledge; they have discarded many of their rites and good conduct of life; they have taken to agriculture as their profession; cruelty has become second nature to them; their ideas have become dirty and defiled.

## \|\| 1.1.18 \|\|

*kṣatriyāśca tathā sarve svadharmatyāgaśīlinaḥ \|*  
*asatsaṃgāḥ pāparatā vyabhicāraparāyaṇāḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Similarly the Kṣatriyas also have discarded their duties; they associate with evil men; they indulge in sinful activities; vice and debauchery have become their main aim in life.

## \|\| 1.1.19 \|\|

*aśūrā araṇaprītāḥ palāyanaparāyaṇāḥ \|*  
*kucauravṛttayaḥ śūdrā ḥ kāmakiṃkaracetasaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. They have ceased to be valorous; they never take interest in virtuous warfare; they flee from the battlefield; they follow the mean tactics of thieves and Śūdras; they are mentally enslaved by base passions.

## \|\| 1.1.20 \|\|

*śastrāstravidyayā hīnā dhenuviprāvanojjhitāḥ \|*  
*śaraṇyāvanahīnāśca kāminyūtimṛgāssadā \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. They have eschewed the practice of miraculous weapons; they never care to protect cows and brahmins; they no longer consider it their duty to protect those who seek refuge in them; they always indulge in brutish sexual dalliance with their damsels.

## \|\| 1.1.21 \|\|

*prajāpālanasaddharmavihīnā bhogatatparāḥ \|*  
*prajāsaṃhārakā duṣṭā jīvahiṃsākarā mudā \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. The good virtue of protecting their subjects they have thrown over-board; they strictly adhere to sensual enjoyment; they are wicked annihilators of their own people; they rejoice in the harassment of all living beings.

## \|\| 1.1.22 \|\|

*vaiśyāḥ saṃskārahīnāste svadharmatyāgaśīlinaḥ \|*  
*kupathāḥ svārjanaratāstulākarmakuvṛttayaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Vaiśyas too no longer perform holy rites; they have cast off their traditional virtue; they have taken to crooked ways to earn more and more; they are now notorious for their malpractices with the weighing balance.

## \|\| 1.1.23 \|\|

*gurudevadvijātīnāṃ bhaktihīnāḥ kubuddhayaḥ \|*  
*abhojitadvijāḥ prāyaḥ kṛpaṇā baddhamuṣṭayaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. They are no longer devoted to preceptors, gods and brahmins; their intellect has become distorted; miserly and tight-fisted they no longer feed the brahmins.

## \|\| 1.1.24 \|\|

*kāminījārabhāveṣu suratā malināśayāḥ \|*  
*lobhamohavicetaskāḥ pūrtādisuvṛṣojjhitāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. They take delight in being the paramours of beautiful women; squalid and filthy in their ideas and deluded by cupidity they have lost clear thinking; they have abandoned their zeal for Pñrta and other holy rites such as digging wells, tanks, planting trees and parks.

## \|\| 1.1.25 \|\|

*tadvacchūdrā śca ye kecidbrāhmaṇācāratatparāḥ \|*  
*ujjvalākṛtayo mūḍhāḥ svadharmatyāgaśīlinaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Similarly most of the Śūdras have become depraved. Some of them show their interest in leading the life of brahmins with shining forms and features; they too in the confusion of their minds have abandoned their traditional practices.

## \|\| 1.1.26 \|\|

*kartārastapasāṃ bhūyo dvijatejopahārakāḥ \|*  
*śiśvalpamṛtyukārāśca maṃtroccāraparāyaṇāḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. In their eagerness to appropriate a brahmanical splendour they frequently perform penances. They cause infantile and premature deaths by their chanting of mantras.

## \|\| 1.1.27 \|\|

*śāligrāmaśilādīnāṃ pūjakāhomatatparāḥ \|*  
*pratikūlavicārāśca kuṭilā dvijadūṣakāḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. They worship the Śālagrāma stone and other things; they evince some interest in Homas too but in their thoughts and actions they are crooked and antagonistic; they calumniate the brahmins.

## \|\| 1.1.28 \|\|

*dhanavaṃtaḥ kukarmmāṇo vidyāvanto vivādinaḥ \|*  
*ākhyāyopāsanā dharmavaktāro dharmalopakāḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. Rich people indulge in misdeeds; learned people take perpetual delight in disputations; those who conduct discourses in holy narratives and expound virtuous rites of worship, themselves abandon virtuous practice of the same.

## \|\| 1.1.29 \|\|

*subhūpākṛtayo daṃbhāḥ sudātāro mahāmadāḥ \|*  
*viprādīnsevakānmatvā manyamānā nijaṃ prabhum \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Haughty persons assume the features of noble kings; those who liberally give, do so with a lot of fuss and haughtiness thinking themselves to be great lords and treating the brahmins and others as their servants.

## \|\| 1.1.30 \|\|

*svadharmarahitā mūḍhāḥ saṃkarāḥ krūrabuddhayaḥ \|*  
*mahābhimānino nityaṃ caturvarṇavilopakāḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. Devoid of the strict observance of their traditional duties and virtues, the foolish people have brought about an admixture of various castes. Cruel in thought and obsessed by false prestiges, people have discarded the four-fold system of social classification.

## \|\| 1.1.31 \|\|

*sukulīnānnijānmatvā caturvarṇairvivartanāḥ \|*  
*sarvavarṇabhraṣṭakarā mūḍhāssatkarmakāriṇaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Deluded people, wrongly considering themselves high-born, perform certain good rites which result only in the upset of the caste-order and down-fall of all people.

## \|\| 1.1.32 \|\|

*striyaśca prāyaśo bhraṣṭā bhartravajñānakārikāḥ \|*  
*śvaśuradro hakāriṇyo nirbhayā malināśanāḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Women too generally misbehave and err; they slight their husbands; they are inimical to their fathers-in-law; fearlessly they pursue their nefarious activities.

## \|\| 1.1.33 \|\|

*kuhāvabhāvaniratāḥ kuśīlāssmaravihvalāḥ \|*  
*jārasaṃgaratā nityaṃ svasvāmivimukhāstathā \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. They indulge in foul coquettish gestures; they are carried away by amorous dispositions; their conduct is bad; they pursue illicit connections with paramours; they turn away from their own husbands.

## \|\| 1.1.34 \|\|

*tanayā mātṛpitrośca bhaktihīnā durāśayāḥ \|*  
*avidyāpāṭhakā nityaṃ rogagrasitadehakāḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. As for sons, they are invariably wicked without any filial affection; they take lessons in ignorant activities and succumb to various ailments.

## \|\| 1.1.35 \|\|

*eteṣāṃ naṣṭabuddhīnāṃ svadharmatyāgaśīlinām \|*  
*paralokepīha loke kathaṃ sūta gatirbhavet \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. O Sūta, how can these deluded people who have abandoned their traditional virtues get salvation here and hereafter.

## \|\| 1.1.36 \|\|

*iti ciṃtākulaṃ cittaṃ jāyate satataṃ hi naḥ \|*  
*paropakārasadṛśo nāsti dharmo paraḥ khalu \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. Hence our minds are always agitated. Indeed there is no virtue equal to helping others.

## \|\| 1.1.37 \|\|

*laghūpāyena yenaiṣāṃ bhavetsadyoghanāśanam \|*  
*sarvvasiddhāntavittvaṃ hi kṛpayā tadvadādhunā \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. Since thou art conversant with the essentials of all tenets, please tell us the easiest remedy for the immediate destruction of the sins of these people.

## \|\| 1.1.38 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*ityākarṇya vacasteṣāṃ munīnāṃ bhāvitātmanām \|*  
*manasā śaṃkaraṃ smṛtvā sūtaḥ provāca tānmunīn \|\| 38 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

38\. On hearing these words of the sages of sanctified souls Sūta thought of Śiva and told them thus.

## Chapter 2 - Answers Clarifying the Doubts of the Sages

## \|\| 1.2.1 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*sādhupṛṣṭaṃ sādhavo vastrailokyahitakārakam \|*  
*guruṃ smṛtvā bhavatsnehādvakṣye tacchṛṇutādarāt \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

1\. O saintly men, the question that you put me is very pertinent. Prompted by my love towards you all I shall, remembering my preceptor, the benefactor of the three worlds, tell you everything. All of you listen attentively.

## \|\| 1.2.2 \|\|

*vedāṃtasārasarvasvaṃ purāṇaṃ śaivamuttamam \|*  
*sarvāghaughoddhārakaraṃ paratra paramārthadam \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. The entire essence of Vedānta is contained in the excellent Śivapurāṇa. It dispells all sins. It affords the attainment of the highest truth (Brahma) hereafter.

## \|\| 1.2.3 \|\|

*kalikalmaṣavidhvaṃsi yasmiñchivayaśaḥ param \|*  
*vijṛmbhate sadā viprāścaturvargaphalapradam \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. O brahmins, the great glory of Śiva, that destroys the sin of the Kali age, unfolds itself in the Purāṇa and yields the fruits of the four varieties (Dharma, Artha, Kāma and Mokṣa).

## \|\| 1.2.4 \|\|

*tasyādhyayanamātreṇa purāṇasya dvijottamāḥ \|*  
*sarvottamasya śaivasya te yāsyaṃti susadgatim \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. By the single-minded study of that most excellent Śivapurāṇa excellent brahmins will attain salvation.

## \|\| 1.2.5 \|\|

*tāvadvijṛṃbhate pāpaṃ brahmahatyāpurassaram \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati jagatyaho \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. It is only as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world, that Brahma-hatyā (the sin of slaying a brahmin) and other sins display themselves.

## \|\| 1.2.6 \|\|

*tāvatkalimahotpātāḥ saṃcariṣyaṃti nirbhayāḥ \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati jagatyaho \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. It is only as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world, that the evil portents of Kali fearlessly roam about.

## \|\| 1.2.7 \|\|

*tāvatsarvāṇi śāstrāṇi vivadaṃti parasparam \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati jagatyaho \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. It is only as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world, that the different sacred texts clash together in disputation.

## \|\| 1.2.8 \|\|

*tāvatsvarūpaṃ durbodhaṃ śivasya mahatāmapi \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi no deṣyati jagatyaho \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. It is difficult even to great men to comprehend Śiva’s features as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world.

## \|\| 1.2.9 \|\|

*tāvadyamabhaṭāḥ krūrāḥ saṃcariṣyaṃti nirbhayāḥ \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati jagatyaho \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. The cruel attendants of Yama roam about fearlessly as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world.

## \|\| 1.2.10 \|\|

*tāvatsarvapurāṇāni pragarjaṃti mahītale \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati jagatyaho \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. All the other Purāṇas roar loudly on the earth as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world.

## \|\| 1.2.11 \|\|

*tāvatsarvāṇi tīrthāni vivadaṃti mahītale \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati jagatyaho \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. All the holy centres enter into mutual wrangles and disputes on the earth as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world.

## \|\| 1.2.12 \|\|

*tāvatsarvāṇi maṃtrāṇi vivadaṃti mahītale \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati mahītale \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. All the mantras rejoice in mutual disputes as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world.

## \|\| 1.2.13 \|\|

*tāvatsarvāṇi kṣetrāṇi vivadaṃti mahītale \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati mahītale \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. All the sectors of pilgrimage engage themselves in mutual disputes as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world.

## \|\| 1.2.14 \|\|

*tāvatsarvāṇi pīṭhāni vivadaṃti mahītale \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati mahītale \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. All the altars and pedestals engage themselves in mutual disputes as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world.

## \|\| 1.2.15 \|\|

*tāvatsarvāṇi dānāni vivadaṃti mahītale \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati mahītale \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. All the gifts engage themselves in disputes as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world.

## \|\| 1.2.16 \|\|

*tāvatsarve ca te devā vivadaṃti mahītale \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati mahītale \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. All those gods engage themselves in mutual disputes as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world.

## \|\| 1.2.17 \|\|

*tāvatsarve ca siddhāntā vivadaṃti mahītale \|*  
*yāvacchivapurāṇaṃ hi nodeṣyati mahītale \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. All the philosophical tenets engage themselves in mutual disputes as long as the Śivapurāṇa has not risen high in the world.

## \|\| 1.2.18 \|\|

*asya śaivapurāṇasya kīrtanaśravaṇāddvijāḥ \|*  
*phalaṃ vaktuṃ na śaknomi kārtsnyena munisattamāḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. O foremost among brahmanical sages, I cannot adequately describe the fruit accruing from reciting and listening to this Śivapurāṇa.

## \|\| 1.2.19 \|\|

*tathāpi tasya māhātmyaṃ vakṣye kiṃcittu vonaghāḥ \|*  
*cittamādhāya śṛṇuta vyāsenoktaṃ purā mama \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Even then, O sinless ones, I shall succinctly describe its greatness as narrated to me by Vyāsa. Please listen attentively.

## \|\| 1.2.20 \|\|

*etacchivapurāṇaṃ hi ślokaṃ ślokārddhameva ca \|*  
*yaḥ paṭhedbhaktisaṃyuktassa pāpānmucyate kṣaṇāt \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. He who reads a single stanza or even half of it piously becomes free from sin instantaneously.

## \|\| 1.2.21 \|\|

*etacchivapurāṇaṃ hi yaḥ pratyahamataṃdri taḥ \|*  
*yathāśakti paṭhedbhaktyā sa jīvanmukta ucyate \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. He who reads every day as much of Śivapurāṇa as he can with devotion and alertness is called Jīvanmukta (a living liberated soul).

## \|\| 1.2.22 \|\|

*etacchivapurāṇaṃ hi yo bhaktyārcayate sadā \|*  
*dine dine'śvamedhasya phalaṃ prāpnotyasaṃśayam \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. He who continues to worship this Śivapurāṇa daily derives the fruit of horse-sacrifice undoubtedly.

## \|\| 1.2.23 \|\|

*etacchivapurāṇaṃ yassādhāraṇapadecchayā \|*  
*anyataḥ śṛṇuyātso'pi matto mucyeta pātakāt \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. He who with a craving for an ordinary position in life listens to Śivapurāṇa even from a person other than me is freed from sin.

## \|\| 1.2.24 \|\|

*etacchivapurāṇaṃ yo namaskuryādadūrataḥ \|*  
*sarvadevārcanaphalaṃ sa prāpnoti na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. He who bows near this Śivapurāṇa derives undoubtedly the fruit of adoration of all the gods.

## \|\| 1.2.25 \|\|

*etacchivapurāṇaṃ vai likhitvā pustakaṃ svayam \|*  
*yo dadyācchivabhaktebhyastasya puṇyaphalaṃ śṛṇu \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Please listen to the meritorious benefit that accrues to the man who copies Śivapurāṇa and gives the manuscript to the devotees of Śiva.

## \|\| 1.2.26 \|\|

*adhīteṣu ca śāstreṣu vedeṣu vyākṛteṣu ca \|*  
*yatphalaṃ durlabhaṃ loke tatphalaṃ tasya saṃbhavet \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. He will have that benefit—very difficult to attain in the world—as that of the study of Śāstras (sacred lore) and of commenting on the Vedas.

## \|\| 1.2.27 \|\|

*etacchivapurāṇaṃ hi caturdaśyāmupoṣitaḥ \|*  
*śivabhaktasabhāyāṃ yo vyākaroti sa uttamaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. He who observes fast on the Caturdaśī (fourteenth day in the lunar fortnight) and conducts discourses and comments on the Śivapurāṇa in the assembly of the devotees of Śiva is the most excellent of all.

## \|\| 1.2.28 \|\|

*pratyakṣaraṃ tu gāyatrīpuraścaryyāphalaṃ labhet \|*  
*iha bhuktvākhilānkāmānaṃ te nirvāṇatāṃ vrajet \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. He shall derive the benefit of the repetition of Gāyatrī syllable by syllable. He will enjoy all worldly pleasures here and attain salvation hereafter.

## \|\| 1.2.29 \|\|

*upoṣitaścaturdaśyāṃ rātrau jāgaraṇānvitaḥ \|*  
*yaḥ paṭhecchṛṇuyādvāpi tasya puṇyaṃ vadāmyaham \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. I shall tell you the benefit derived by him who reads or listens to this after observing fast on the Caturdaśī day by keeping awake in the night.

## \|\| 1.2.30-31 \|\|

*kurukṣetrādinikhilapuṇyatīrtheṣvanekaśaḥ \|*  
*ātmatulyadhanaṃ sūryyagrahaṇe sarvatomukhe \|\| 30 \|\|*

*viprebhyo vyāsamukhyebhyo dattvāyatphalamaśnute \|*  
*tatphalaṃ saṃbhavettasya satyaṃ satyaṃ na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

30-31. This is the truth, undoubtedly the truth that he will get the benefit derived by the man who makes gifts of wealth equal in weight to himself to brahmins with Vyāsas at their head at the complete eclipse of the sun, many a time, in all holy centres, Kurukṣetra31 etc.

## \|\| 1.2.32 \|\|

*etacchivapurāṇaṃ hi gāyate yopyaharniśam \|*  
*ājñāṃ tasya pratīkṣerandevā indra puro gamāḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Indra and other devas wait eagerly for the directives of the man who chants day and night the verses of the Śiva-purāṇa.

## \|\| 1.2.33 \|\|

*etacchivapurāṇaṃ yaḥ paṭhañchṛṇvanhi nityaśaḥ \|*  
*yadyatkaroti satkarma tatkoṭiguṇitaṃ bhavet \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. The sacred rites performed by the man who regularly reads or listens to the Śivapurāṇa are effective millions of times more than usual.

## \|\| 1.2.34 \|\|

*samāhitaḥ paṭhedyastu tatra śrīrudra saṃhitām \|*  
*sa brahmaghno'pi pūtātmā tribhirevādinairbhavet \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. He who reads the Rudrasaṃhitā portion of Śiva-purāṇa with pure and concentrated mind becomes a purified soul within three days even though he might have killed a brahmin.

## \|\| 1.2.35 \|\|

*tāṃ rudra saṃhitāṃ yastu bhairavapratimāṃtike \|*  
*triḥ paṭhetpratyahaṃ maunī sa kāmānakhilāṃ llabhet \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. He who reads the Rudrasaṃhitā three times a day near the image of Bhairava, refraining from useless talk, shall get all cherished desires fulfilled.

## \|\| 1.2.36 \|\|

*tāṃ rudra saṃhitāṃ yastu sapaṭhedvaṭabilvayoḥ \|*  
*pradakṣiṇāṃ prakurvāṇo brahmahatyā nivartate \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. If a slayer of brahmin circumambulates the trees of Vaṭa and Bilva reciting the verses from Rudrasaṃhitā he will become purified of the sin of Brahmin-slaughter.

## \|\| 1.2.37 \|\|

*kailāsasaṃhitā tatra tato'pi paramasmṛtā \|*  
*brahmasvarūpiṇī sākṣātpraṇavārthaprakāśikā \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. The Kailāsa saṃhitā is even greater than that. It is of Vedic status and stature. The meaning of Praṇava (the sacred syllable Om) is amplified in it.

## \|\| 1.2.38 \|\|

*kailāsasaṃhitāyāstu māhātmyaṃ vetti śaṃkaraḥ \|*  
*kṛtsnaṃ tadarddhaṃ vyāsaśca tadarddhaṃ vedmyahaṃ dvijāḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. O Brahmins, Lord Śiva knows the greatness of Kailāśasaṃhitā in its entirety. Vyāsa knows half of it and I a moiety of the same.

## \|\| 1.2.39 \|\|

*tatra kiṃcitpravakṣyāmi kṛtsnaṃ vaktuṃ na śakyate \|*  
*yajjñātvā tatkṣaṇāllokaścittaśuddhimavāpnuyāt \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. A part of it, I shall tell you, since it is impossible to say everything. On comprehending it people attain purity of their minds instantaneously.

## \|\| 1.2.40 \|\|

*na nāśayati yatpāpaṃ sā raudrī saṃhitā dvijāḥ \|*  
*tanna paśyāmyahaṃ loke mārgamāṇo'pi sarvadā \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. O Brahmins, seeking for it ever and anon, I do not see a sin that cannot be quelled by Rudrasaṃhitā.

## \|\| 1.2.41 \|\|

*śivenopaniṣatsiṃdhumanthanotpāditāṃ mudā \|*  
*kumārāyārpitāṃ tāṃ vai sudhāṃ pītvā'maro bhavet \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. Drinking that nectar prepared by Lord Śiva after churning the ocean of the Upaniṣads (a class of Vedic literature) and handed over to Kumāra (Lord Kārtikeya) the devotee shall become immortal.

## \|\| 1.2.42 \|\|

*brahmahatyādipāpānāṃ niṣkṛtiṃ kartumudyataḥ \|*  
*māsamātraṃ saṃhitāṃ tāṃ paṭhitvā mucyate tataḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. The person intending to perform expiatory rites for the sins of Brahma-hatyā etc. should read that Saṃhitā for a month. He shall be freed of that sin.

## \|\| 1.2.43 \|\|

*duṣpratigrahadurbhojyadurālāpādisaṃbhavam \|*  
*pāpaṃ sakṛtkīrtanena saṃhitā sā vināśayet \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. By a single recital, that Saṃhitā destroys the sin originating from the acceptance of monetary gifts from defiled persons, partaking of defiled food and indulging in foul talks.

## \|\| 1.2.44 \|\|

*śivālaye bilvavane saṃhitāṃ tāṃ paṭhettu yaḥ \|*  
*sa tatphalamavāpnoti yadvāco'pi na gocare \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. The benefit derived by a person who reads that Saṃhitā in the grove of Bilva trees in a temple of Śiva is beyond description in words.

## \|\| 1.2.45 \|\|

*saṃhitāṃ tāṃ paṭhanbhaktyā yaḥ śrāddhe bhojayeddvijān \|*  
*tasya ye pitaraḥ sarve yāṃti śaṃbhoḥ paraṃ padam \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. If a person reads that Saṃhitā with devotion at the time of performing Śrāddha and feeding the brahmins, all his Pitṛs (manes) attain the great region of Śiva.

## \|\| 1.2.46 \|\|

*caturdaśyāṃ nirāhāro yaḥ paṭhetsaṃhitāṃ ca tām \|*  
*bilvamūle śivaḥ sākṣātsa devaiśca prapūjyate \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. The devotee who observes fast on the Caturdaśī day and reads that Saṃhitā under the Bilva tree is directly identified with Śiva and is worshipped by the gods.

## \|\| 1.2.47 \|\|

*anyāpi saṃhitā tatra sarvakāmaphalapradā \|*  
*ubhe viśiṣṭe vijñeye līlāvijñānapūrite \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. The other Saṃhitās are no doubt the bestowers of the benefit of fulfilling all cherished desires. These two Saṃhitās are particularly excellent as they are full of divine sports and divine knowledge.

## \|\| 1.2.48 \|\|

*tadidaṃ śaivamākhyātaṃ purāṇaṃ vedasaṃmitam \|*  
*nirmitaṃ tacchivenaiva prathamaṃ brahmasaṃmitam \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. Such is the Śivapurāṇa, extolled on a par with the Vedas, created by Lord Śiva Himself at first and commensurate with the supreme Brahman.

## \|\| 1.2.49-51 \|\|

*vidyeśaṃca tathāraudraṃ vaināyakamathaumikam \|*  
*mātraṃ rudrai kādaśakaṃ kailāsaṃ śatarudra kam \|\| 49 \|\|*

*koṭirudra sahasrādyaṃ koṭirudraṃ tathaiva ca \|*  
*vāyavīyaṃ dharmasaṃjñaṃ purāṇamiti bhedataḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

*saṃhitā dvādaśamitā mahāpuṇyatarā matā \|*  
*tāsāṃ saṃkhyāṃ bruve viprāḥ śṛṇutādaratokhilam \|\| 51 \|\|*

49-51. Originally the Śivapurāṇa was of very enormous size consisting of twelve sacred Saṃhitās:—(1) Vidyeśvara (2) Rudra, (3) Vaināyaka, (4) Aumika, (5) Mātṛ (6) Rudraikādaśa, (7) Kailāsa, (8) Śatarudraka, (9) Sahasrakoṭirudra, (10) Koṭirudra, (11) Vāyavīya and (12) Dharmasaṃjña. O brahmins, I shall mention the number of verses in those Saṃhitās. Please listen with due attention.

## \|\| 1.2.52 \|\|

*vidyeśaṃ daśasāhasraṃ rudraṃ vaināyakaṃ tathā \|*  
*aumaṃ mātṛpurāṇākhyaṃ pratyekāṣṭasahasrakam \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. The first Saṃhitā of Vidyeśvara, consisted of ten thousand verses. The Raudra, Vaināyaka, Aumika and Mātṛ Saṃhitās consisted of eight thousand verses each.

## \|\| 1.2.53 \|\|

*trayodaśasahasraṃ hi rudrai kādaśakaṃ dvijāḥ \|*  
*ṣaṭsahasraṃ ca kailāsaṃ śatarudraṃ tadarddhakam \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. O brahmins, the Rudraikādasa saṃhitā consisted of thirteen thousand verses; the Kailāsa saṃhitā of six thousand verses and the Śatarudra of three thousand verses.

## \|\| 1.2.54 \|\|

*koṭirudraṃ triguṇitamekādaśasahasrakam \|*  
*sahasrakoṭirudrā khyamuditaṃ graṃthasaṃkhyayā \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. The Koṭirudra saṃhitā consisted of nine thousand verses; the Sahasrakoṭi-Rudra saṃhitā of eleven thousand verses.

## \|\| 1.2.55 \|\|

*vāyavīyaṃ khābdhiśataṃ gharmaṃ ravisahasrakam \|*  
*tadevaṃ lakṣasaṃkhyākaṃ śaivasaṃkhyāvibhedataḥ \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. The Vāyavīya saṃhitā consisted of 4000 verses and the Dharma saṃhitā of twelve thousand verses. Thus the whole Śivapurāṇa contained a hundred thousand verses.

## \|\| 1.2.56 \|\|

*vyāsena tattu saṃkṣiptaṃ caturviṃśatsahasrakam \|*  
*śaivaṃ tatra caturthaṃ vai purāṇaṃ saptasaṃhitam \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. That has been condensed by Vyāsa to twenty-four thousand verses; that is to about a fourth of the original Purāṇa and he retained seven saṃhitās.

## \|\| 1.2.57 \|\|

*śive saṃkalpitaṃ pūrvaṃ purāṇaṃ granthasaṃkhyayā \|*  
*śatakoṭipramāṇaṃ hi purā sṛṣṭau suvismṛtam \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. The Purāṇic lore at the time of the first creation as conceived by Śiva contained a thousand million (hundred crores) verses.

## \|\| 1.2.58 \|\|

*vyasteṣṭādaśadhā caiva purāṇe dvāparādiṣu \|*  
*caturlakṣeṇa saṃkṣipte kṛte dvaipāyanādibhiḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. In the Kṛta age Dvaipāyana and others condensed it into four hundred thousand verses which in the beginning of Dvāpara age was separated into eighteen different Purāṇas.

## \|\| 1.2.59 \|\|

*proktaṃ śivapurāṇaṃ hi caturviṃśatsahasrakam \|*  
*ślokānāṃ saṃkhyayā saptasaṃhitaṃ brahmasaṃmitam \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. Of these the Śivapurāṇa contains twenty-four thousand verses with seven Saṃhitās and the Purāṇa is on a par with the Vedas (in excellence).

## \|\| 1.2.60 \|\|

*vidyeśvarākhyā tatrādyā raudrī jñeyā dvitīyikā \|*  
*tṛtīyā śatarudrā khyā koṭirudrā caturthikā \|\| 60 \|\|*

60\. The first Saṃhitā is called Vidyeśvara, the second Rudra, the third Śatarudra and the fourth Koṭirudra.

## \|\| 1.2.61 \|\|

*paṃcamī caiva maumākhyā ṣaṣṭhī kailāsasaṃjñikā \|*  
*saptamī vāyavīyākhyā saptaivaṃ saṃhitāmatāḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

61\. The fifth is Aumi (of Umā), the sixth Kailāsa and the seventh Vāyavīya; these are the seven Saṃhitās.

## \|\| 1.2.62 \|\|

*sasaptasaṃhitaṃ divyaṃ purāṇaṃ śivasaṃjñakam \|*  
*varīvarti brahmatulyaṃ sarvopari gatipradam \|\| 62 \|\|*

62\. Thus the divine Śivapurāṇa with its seven Saṃhitās stands on a par with the Vedas, according salvation more than anything else.

## \|\| 1.2.63 \|\|

*etacchivapurāṇaṃ hi saptasaṃhitamādarāt \|*  
*paripūrṇaṃ paṭhedyastu sa jīvanmukta ucyate \|\| 63 \|\|*

63\. He who reads this Śivapurāṇa complete with the seven Saṃhitās devotedly is a living liberated soul.

## \|\| 1.2.64 \|\|

*śrutismṛtipurāṇetihāsāgamaśatāni ca \|*  
*etacchivapurāṇasya nārhaṃtyalpāṃ kalāmapi \|\| 64 \|\|*

64\. Hundreds of other sacred texts as the Vedas, Smṛtis, Purāṇas, Itihāsas, and Āgamas do not merit even a sixteenth of this Śivapurāṇa.

## \|\| 1.2.65 \|\|

*śaivaṃ purāṇamamalaṃ śivakīrtitaṃ tadvyāsena śaivapravaṇena na saṃgṛhītam \|*  
*saṃkṣepataḥ sakalajīvaguṇopakāre tāpatrayaghnamatulaṃ śivadaṃ satāṃ hi \|\| 65 \|\|*

65\. Śivapurāṇa is first expounded by Śiva and then condensed by Vyāsa, a devotee of Śiva. It is pure and brief and as such it renders help to all living beings. As a queller of the threefold calamities (physical, extraneous and divine) it is unrivalled. It bestows welfare upon the good.

## \|\| 1.2.66-67 \|\|

*vikaitavo dharma iha pragīto vedāṃtavijñānamayaḥ pradhānaḥ \|*  
*amatsarāṃtarbudhavedyavastu satkḷptamatraiva trivargayuktam \|\| 66 \|\|*

*śaivaṃ purāṇatilakaṃ khalu satpurāṇaṃ \|*  
*vedāṃtavedavilasatparavastugītam \|*  
*yo vai paṭhecca śṛṇuyātparamādareṇa śaṃbhupriyaḥ sa hi labhetparamāṃ \|*  
*gatiṃ vai \|\| 67 \|\|*

66-67. Undeceptive virtue is extolled herein; it is, in the main, of the nature of Vedantic wisdom. It contains mantras, and three aims of life and the thing knowable by wise men of unprejudiced mind. The Śivapurāṇa is the best among the Purāṇas, extolling the great Being that glows in Vedānta and the Vedas. He who reads and listens to it with devotion becomes a favourite of Śiva and attains the supreme position (here and hereafter).

## Chapter 3 - The deliberation on the achievable and the means of achievement

## \|\| 1.3.1 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*ityākarṇya vacaḥ sautaṃ procuste paramarṣayaḥ \|*  
*vedāṃtasārasarvasvaṃ purāṇaṃ śrāvayādbhutam \|\| 1 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

1\. On hearing the words of Sūta, the great sages said, “Please narrate the wonderful Purāṇa that fully treats of the essence of Vedānta”.

## \|\| 1.3.2 \|\|

*iti śrutvā munīnāṃ sa vacanaṃ supraharṣitaḥ \|*  
*saṃsmarañchaṃkaraṃ sūtaḥ provāca munisattamān \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. Very delighted at the request of the sages Sūta meditated on Śiva and spoke to them.

## \|\| 1.3.3 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇvaṃtu ṛṣayaḥ sarve smṛtvā śivamanāmayam \|*  
*purāṇapravaṇaṃ śaivaṃ purāṇaṃ vedasārajam \|\| 3 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

3\. Contemplating on Śiva free from ailments may ye all hear this Śivapurāṇa, the foremost among Purāṇas, that amplifies the essence of the Vedas.

## \|\| 1.3.4-5 \|\|

*yatra gītaṃ trikaṃ prītyā bhaktijñānavirāgakam \|\| 4 \|\|*

*vedāṃtavedyaṃ sadvastu viśeṣeṇa pravarṇitam \|\| 5 \|\|*

4-5. Where the trio, Bhakti (Piety) Jñāna (Wisdom) and Vairāgya (non-attachment) has been proclaimed and the object which is knowable only through Vedānta, has been particularly described.

## \|\| 1.3.6-8 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇvaṃtu ṛṣayaḥ sarve purāṇaṃ vedasārajam \|*  
*purā kālena mahatā kalpe'tīte punaḥpunaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

*asminnupasthite kalpe pravṛtte sṛṣṭikarmaṇi \|*  
*munīnāṃ ṣaṭkulīnānāṃ bruvatāmitaretaram \|\| 7 \|\|*

*idaṃ paramidaṃ neti vivādaḥ sumahānabhūt \|*  
*te'bhijagmurvidhātāraṃ brahmāṇaṃ praṣṭumavyayam \|\| 8 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

6-8. May ye all hear the Purāṇa that embibes the essence of the Vedas. Formerly, when many Kalpas (Aeons) elapsed and this Kalpa started with the process of creation, a great dispute arose among the sages of six clans who held divergent views as to which is great and which is not. They approached Brahmā the Creator, to ask him about the imperishable.

## \|\| 1.3.9-12 \|\|

*vāgbhirvinayagarbhābhiḥ sarve prāṃjalayo'bruvan \|*  
*tvaṃ hi sarvajagaddhātā sarvakāraṇakāraṇam \|\| 9 \|\|*

*kaḥ pumānsarvatattvebhyaḥ purāṇaḥ parataḥ paraḥ \|*  
*brahmovāca \|*  
*yato vāco nivartaṃte aprāpya manasā saha \|\| 10 \|\|*

*yasmātsarvamidaṃ brahmaviṣṇurudre draṃ pūrvakam \|*  
*sahabhūteṃdri yaiḥ sarvaiḥ prathamaṃ saṃprasūyate \|\| 11 \|\|*

*eṣa devo mahādevaḥ sarvajño jagadīśvaraḥ \|*  
*ayaṃ tu parayā bhaktyā dṛśyate nā'nyathā kvacit \|\| 12 \|\|*

9-12. All of them with palms joined in reverence addressed him with words couched in humility—“Thou art the creator of the entire universe, the cause of all causes. Who is that Being older than all Principles, the greatest of the great?

## \|\| 1.3.13 \|\|

*rudro harirharaścaiva tathānye ca sureśvarāḥ \|*  
*bhaktyā paramayā tasya nityaṃ darśanakāṃkṣiṇaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Brahmā said:—

“That from whom words recede, not approaching him even with the mind; that from whom this entire universe beginning with Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Rudra and Indra, along with all elements and all sense-organs, is evolved at first; he is the lord Mahādeva the omniscient, the lord of the universe. He can be realised by supreme devotion and not by other means.

13\. Rudra, Hari, Hara and other lords of Devas are ever desirous of seeing Him, moved by great devotion.

## \|\| 1.3.14 \|\|

*bahunātra kimuktena śive bhaktyā vimucyate \|*  
*prasādāddevatābhaktiḥ prasādo bhaktisaṃbhavaḥ \|*  
*yathehāṃkurato bījaṃ bījato vā yathāṃkuraḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Of what avail is a verbose statement? One is liberated by devotion unto Śiva. Devotion to the deity is due to His Grace; and His grace is due to devotion just as the seed gives rise to the sprout and the sprout produces the seed.

## \|\| 1.3.15 \|\|

*tasmādīśaprasādārthaṃ yūyaṃ gatvā bhuvaṃ dvijāḥ \|*  
*dīrghasatraṃ samākṛdhvaṃ yūyaṃ varṣasahasrakam \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Hence, O Brahmins, all of you descend to the earth, to propitiate the Lord. You have to perform a sacrifice of long duration for a thousand years.

## \|\| 1.3.16 \|\|

*amuṣyaivādhvareśasya śivasyaiva prasādataḥ \|*  
*vedoktavidyāsāraṃ tu jñāyate sādhyasādhanaṃ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. It is by the grace of Śiva alone who will be the presiding deity of this sacrifice that the means of achievement of the Achievable can be realised and that is the essence of the Vidyā (mystic learning) mentioned in the Vedas.

## \|\| 1.3.17 \|\|

*munaya ūcuḥ \|*  
*atha kiṃ paramaṃ sādhyaṃ kiṃvā tatsādhanaṃ param \|*  
*sādhakaḥ kīdṛśastatra tadidaṃ brūhi tattvataḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

The sages said:—

17\. What is that great Achievable? What is that great means of achievement? Of what sort is the performer of the rite? Please mention these precisely.

## \|\| 1.3.18 \|\|

*brahmovāca \|*  
*sādhyaṃ śivapadaprāptiḥ sādhanaṃ tasya sevanam \|*  
*sādhakastatprasādādyo'nityādiphalaniḥspṛhaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Brahmā said:—

18\. The attainment of Śiva’s region is the Achievable. Means of achievement is the service rendered unto Him. Sādhaka (the performer of the rite) is the person who is free from desire even for permanence which attitude is the result of His grace.

## \|\| 1.3.19 \|\|

*karma kṛtvā tu vedoktaṃ tadarpitamahāphalam \|*  
*parameśapadaprāptaḥ sālokyādikramāttataḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Rites mentioned in the Vedas should be performed with the fruits thereof dedicated to Him. Thence, through Sālokya he attains the feet of the great Lord.

## \|\| 1.3.20 \|\|

*tattadbhaktyanusāreṇa sarveṣāṃ paramaṃ phalam \|*  
*tatsādhanaṃ bahuvidhaṃ sākṣādīśena bodhitam \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. All attain the great fruit according to the standard in devotion achieved. The ways of achieving these standards are manifold as expounded by Isa Himself.

## \|\| 1.3.21-22 \|\|

*saṃkṣipya tatra vaḥ sāraṃ sādhanaṃ prabravīmyaham \|*  
*śrotreṇa śravaṇaṃ tasya vacasā kīrtanaṃ tathā \|\| 21 \|\|*

*manasā mananaṃ tasya mahāsādhanamucyate \|*  
*śrotavyaḥ kīrtitavyaśca mantavyaśca maheśvaraḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

21-22. I shall condense the same and tell you the essential means. Listening to the glory of Śiva, glorifying him by means of words, and deliberation in the mind, these constitute the greatest of the means. Maheśvara is to be heard, glorified and meditated upon.

## \|\| 1.3.23 \|\|

*iti śrutipramāṇaṃ naḥ sādhanenā'munā param \|*  
*sādhyaṃ vrajata sarvārthasādhanaikaparāyaṇāḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Thus Śruti is our authority. Resorting solely to this great means, all of you attain the Achievable.

## \|\| 1.3.24 \|\|

*pratyakṣaṃ cakṣuṣā dṛṣṭvā tatra lokaḥ pravartate \|*  
*apratyakṣaṃ hi sarvatra jñātvā śrotreṇa ceṣṭate \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. Regarding visible things people see with their eyes and begin their activity. Concerning the invisible everywhere, they know through the ears and activise themselves.

## \|\| 1.3.25 \|\|

*tasmācchravaṇamevādau śrutvā gurumukhādbudhaḥ \|*  
*tataḥ saṃsādhayedanyatkīrtanaṃ mananaṃ sudhīḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Hence Śravaṇa (listening) is the first rite. The intelligent scholar must listen to the oral explanation of the preceptor and then practise the other rites.—Kīrtana (glorifying) and Manana (deliberation).

## \|\| 1.3.26-27 \|\|

*kramānmananaparyaṃte sādhane'sminsusādhite \|*  
*śivayogo bhavettena sālokyādikramācchanaiḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

*sarvāṃgavyādhayaḥ paścātsarvānaṃdaśca līyate \|*  
*abhyāsātkleśametadvai paścādādyaṃtamaṃgalam \|\| 27 \|\|*

26-27. When all the means upto Manana are well exercised, Śivayoga (unification with Śiva) results gradually through Sālokya etc. All the ailments of the body are nullified and supreme bliss is realised. Painful indeed is the process but later on everything becomes auspicious from beginning to end.

## Chapter 4 - The Excellence of Listening and Deliberation

## \|\| 1.4.1 \|\|

*munaya ūcuḥ \|*  
*mananaṃ kīdṛśaṃ brahmañchravaṇaṃ cāpi kīdṛśam \|*  
*kīrtanaṃ vā kathaṃ tasya kīrtayaitadyathāyatham \|\| 1 \|\|*

The sages said:—

1\. O holy one, what is Śravaṇa? what is Manana? How is the Kīrtana performed? Please expound these precisely.

## \|\| 1.4.2 \|\|

*brahmovaca \|*  
*pūjājapeśaguṇarūpavilāsanāmnāṃ yuktipriyeṇa manasā pariśodhanaṃ yat \|*  
*tatsaṃtataṃ mananamīśvaradṛṣṭilabhyaṃ sarveṣu sādhanavareṣvapi mukhyamukhyam \|\| 2 \|\|*

Brahmā said:—

2\. The mind is fond of reasoning deliberation. The ability of the mind to ponder and evaluate the corresponding efficacy of the worship, Japa, the attributes of Īśa, His form, His divine sports and multifarious names, is the result of the benignant glance of Īśvara. Hence this steady continuance in the act of deliberation is the most important of all the means.

## \|\| 1.4.3 \|\|

*gītātmanā śrutipadena ca bhāṣayā vā śaṃbhupratāpaguṇarūpavilāsanāmnām \|*  
*vācā sphuṭaṃ tu rasavatstavanaṃ yadasya tatkīrtanaṃ bhavati sādhanamatra madhyam \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. By Kīrtana (glorification) is meant the clear expression of Śiva’s exploits, attributes, forms, sports, names etc. in good taste by reciting traditional lore, singing songs of praise even in mother tongue. It is the middle one of the three means.

## \|\| 1.4.4 \|\|

*yenāpi kena karaṇena ca śabdapuṃjaṃ yatra kvacicchivaparaṃ śravaṇeṃdri yeṇa \|*  
*strīkelivaddṛḍhataraṃ praṇidhīyate yattadvai budhāḥ śravaṇamatra jagatprasiddham \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. O wise men, the means of Śravaṇa famous in the world is the listening to words concerning Śiva, in whatever manner, howsoever and wherever they are produced with the same steady attention as in the sporting dalliance of women.

## \|\| 1.4.5 \|\|

*satsaṃgamena bhavati śravaṇaṃ purastātsaṃkīrtanaṃ paśupateratha taddṛḍhaṃ syāt \|*  
*sarvottamaṃ bhavati tanmananaṃ tadaṃte sarvaṃ hi saṃbhavati śaṃkaradṛṣṭipāte \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Śravaṇa (listening) is effected when one associates with good men. Then the Kīrtana of Paśupati becomes steady. In the end is the Manana which is the most excellent. All these take place as a result of benevolent surveillance of Lord Śiva.

## \|\| 1.4.6 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*asminsādhanamāhatmye purā vṛttaṃ munīśvarāḥ \|*  
*yuṣmadarthaṃ pravakṣyāmi śṛṇudhvamavadhānataḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

6\. O saints, in the context of the elucidation of the greatness of the means, I shall narrate an anecdote of former days for your sake. Please listen to them attentively.

## \|\| 1.4.7 \|\|

*purā mama gururvyāsaḥ parāśaramuneḥ sutaḥ \|*  
*tapaścacāra saṃbhrāṃtaḥ sarasvatyāstaṭe śubhe \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Long ago, my preceptor Vyāsa, the son of Sage Parāśara, performed penance on the bank of the river Sarasvatī with some mental agitation.

## \|\| 1.4.8 \|\|

*gacchanyadṛchayā tatra vimānenārkarociṣā \|*  
*sanatkumāro bhagavāndadarśa mama deśikam \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. The divine sage Sanatkumāra who happened to go that way in an aerial chariot resplendent like the sun, espied my preceptor.

## \|\| 1.4.9 \|\|

*dhyānārūḍhaḥ prabuddho'sau dadarśa tamajātmajam \|*  
*praṇipatyāha saṃbhrāṃtaḥ paraṃ kautūhalaṃ muniḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Waking up from his meditation my preceptor saw the son of Brahmā. The sage thereupon paid obeisance in a flutter and eagerness.

## \|\| 1.4.10 \|\|

*dattvārghyamasmai pradadau devayogyaṃ ca viṣṭiram \|*  
*prasannaḥ prāha taṃ prahvaṃ prabhurgaṃbhīrayā girā \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. He offered Arghya and a seat befitting the divinity of the sage. Being delighted, the divine sage spoke to my humble preceptor in words of great profundity.

## \|\| 1.4.11 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*satyaṃ vastu mune dadhyāḥ sākṣātkaraṇagocaraḥ \|*  
*sa śivothāsahāyotra tapaścarasi kiṃ kṛte \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. O sage, you must meditate upon the True object. The great lord Śiva can be realised and seen. But wherefore do you perform the penance here unattended?

## \|\| 1.4.12-14 \|\|

*evamuktaḥ kumāreṇa provāca svāśayaṃ muniḥ \|*  
*dharmārthakāmamokṣāśca vedamārge kṛtādarāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

*bahudhā sthāpitā loke mayā tvatkṛpayā tathā \|*  
*evaṃ bhutasya mepyevaṃ gurubhūtasya sarvataḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

*muktisādhanakaṃ jñānaṃ nodeti paramādbhutam \|*  
*tapaścarāmi muktyarthaṃ na jāne tatra kāraṇam \|\| 14 \|\|*

12-14. When Sanatkumāra addressed him thus, the sage Vyāsa clarified his purpose. “By the favour of divine elders like you I have almost established the four ways of virtue, wealth, love and salvation with due adherence to the Vedic path, in the world. I have become a preceptor unto all. Still it is surprising that the knowledge of the means of liberation has not dawned on me. I am performing penance for the sake of salvation. But I do not know how it can be achieved.

## \|\| 1.4.15 \|\|

*itthaṃ kumāro bhagavānvyāsena muninārthitaḥ \|*  
*samarthaḥ prāha vipreṃdrā niścayaṃ muktikāraṇam \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. O excellent brahmins, when thus requested by the sage Vyāsa, the competent divine sage Sanatkumāra told him of the sure way of realising salvation.

## \|\| 1.4.16 \|\|

*śravaṇaṃ kīrtanaṃ śaṃbhormananaṃ ca mahattaram \|*  
*trayaṃ sādhanamuktaṃ ca vidyate vedasaṃmatam \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. It has already been mentioned that there are three means in conformity with Vedic ideal viz. Śravaṇa, Kīrtana and the highly efficacious Manana of Śiva.

## \|\| 1.4.17 \|\|

*purāhamatha saṃbhrāṃto hyanyasādhanasaṃbhramaḥ \|*  
*acale maṃdare śaile tapaścaraṇamācaram \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. Formerly, I too, confounded by other means performed a great penance on the mountain Mandara.

## \|\| 1.4.18-19 \|\|

*śivājñayā tataḥ prāpto bhagavānnaṃdikeśvaraḥ \|*  
*sa me dayālurbhagavānsarvasākṣī gaṇeśvaraḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

*uvāca mahyaṃ sasnehaṃ muktisādhanamuttamam \|*  
*śravaṇaṃ kīrtanaṃ śaṃbhormananaṃ vedasaṃmatam \|\| 19 \|\|*

18-19. At the bidding of Śiva, the divine attendant Nandikeśvara arrived there. That sympathetic lord of Gaṇas, witness of all, lovingly told me about the excellent means of salvation. Viz.—Śravaṇa, Kīrtana and Manana all in conformity with Vedic ideals.

## \|\| 1.4.20 \|\|

*trikaṃ ca sādhanaṃ muktau śivena mama bhāṣitam \|*  
*śravaṇādiṃ trikaṃ brahmankuruṣveti muhurmuhuḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Hence, O holy sage, as advised by Śiva these are the three means of salvation. Please practise them.” He repeatedly advised Vyāsa thus.

## \|\| 1.4.21 \|\|

*evamuktvā tato vyāsaṃ sānugo vidhinaṃdanaḥ \|*  
*jagāma svavimānena padaṃ paramaśobhanam \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. After saying this to Vyāsa, the son of Brahmā mounted the aerial chariot accompanied by his followers and returned to his splendid and auspicious region.

## \|\| 1.4.22-23 \|\|

*evamuktaṃ samāsena pūrvavṛttāṃtamuttamam \|*  
*ṛṣaya ūcuḥ \|*  
*śravaṇāditrayaṃ sūta muktyopāyastvayeritaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

*śravaṇāditrike'śaktaḥ kiṃ kṛtvā mucyate janaḥ \|*  
*ayatnenaiva muktiḥ syātkarmaṇā kena hetunā \|\| 23 \|\|*

22-23. Thus, in brief, I have told you the ancient anecdote.

## Chapter 5 - The greatness of the phallic emblem (liṅga) of Śiva

## \|\| 1.5.1 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*śravaṇāditrike'śakto liṃgaṃ beraṃ ca śāṃkaram \|*  
*saṃsthāpya nityamabhyarcya taretsaṃsārasāgaram \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

1\. A person incompetent to perform the three rites of Śravaṇa etc. shall fix the phallic emblem (liṅga) or the image of Śiva and worship them every day. He can thus cross the ocean of worldly existence.

## \|\| 1.5.2 \|\|

*api dra vyaṃ vahedeva yathābalamavaṃcayan \|*  
*arpayelliṃgaberārthamarcayedapi saṃtatam \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. As far as he can afford, the devotee shall make gifts of wealth too without deceiving others. He shall offer them to the phallic emblem or the image of Śiva. He must worship them constantly.

## \|\| 1.5.3-7 \|\|

*maṃḍapaṃ gopuraṃ tīrthaṃ maṭhaṃ kṣetraṃ tathotsavam \|*  
*vastraṃ gaṃdhaṃ ca mālyaṃ ca dhūpaṃ dīpaṃ ca bhaktitaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

*vividhānnaṃ ca naivedyamapūpavyaṃjanairyutam \|*  
*chatraṃ dhvajaṃ ca vyajanaṃ cāmaraṃ cāpi sāṃgakam \|\| 4 \|\|*

*rājopacāravatsarvaṃ dhārayelliṃgaberayoḥ \|*  
*pradakṣiṇāṃ namaskāraṃ yathāśakti japaṃ tathā \|\| 5 \|\|*

*āvāhanādisargāṃtaṃ nityaṃ kuryātsubhaktitaḥ \|*  
*itthamabhyarcya yandevaṃ liṃgebere ca śāṃkare \|\| 6 \|\|*

*siddhimeti śivaprītyā hitvāpi śravaṇādikam \|*  
*liṃgaberārcanāmātrānmuktāḥ purve mahājanāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

3-7. The worship must be performed elaborately. Construction of platforms, ornamental portals, monasteries, temples, holy centres, etc., offerings of cloth, scents, garlands, incense, lamps, with due piety; oblations of various cooked rice, pancakes, pies etc. with side dishes; umbrellas, fans, chowries with all paraphernalia—everything shall be maintained in the worship of Śiva. In fact, all royal homage shall be paid. Circumambulation and obeisance with Japas according to capacity shall be performed. All the different usual rites in worships like invocation shall be maintained with due devotion. A person who worships the phallic emblem or the image in this manner will attain salvation even without Śravaṇa etc. Many noble men of yore have been Uberated solely by this simple worship,

## \|\| 1.5.8 \|\|

*manuya ūcuḥ \|*  
*beramātre tu sarvatra pūjyaṃte devatāgaṇāḥ \|*  
*liṃgebere ca sarvatra kathaṃ saṃpūjyate śivaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Everywhere the deities are worshipped only in their image. How is it that Śiva is worshipped both in the image and the phallus?

## \|\| 1.5.9 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*aho munīśvarāḥ puṇyaṃ praśnametanmahādbhutam \|*  
*atra vaktā mahādevo nānyo'sti puruṣaḥ kvacit \|\| 9 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

9\. O sages, this question is holy and wondrous. Here the speaker is Śiva Himself and not any ordinary person.

## \|\| 1.5.10 \|\|

*śivenoktaṃ pravakṣyāmi kramādgurumukhācchrutam \|*  
*śivaiko brahmarūpatvānniṣkalaḥ parikīrtitaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. I shall tell you what Śiva Himself had said and what I heard from my own preceptor. Śiva alone is glorified as Niṣkala (nameless and formless) since He is identical with supreme Brahman.

## \|\| 1.5.11 \|\|

*rūpitvātsakalastadvattasmātsakalaniṣkalaḥ \|*  
*niṣkalatvānnirākāraṃ liṃgaṃ tasya samāgatam \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. He is also Sakala as He has an embodied form. He is both Sakala and Niṣkala. It is in his Niṣkala aspect that the Liṅga is appropriate.

## \|\| 1.5.12-13 \|\|

*sakalatvāttathā beraṃ sākāraṃ tasya saṃgatam \|*  
*sakalākalarūpatvādbrahmaśabdābhidhaḥ paraḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

*api liṃge ca bere ca nityamabhyarcyate janaiḥ \|*  
*abrahmatvāttadanyeṣāṃ niṣkalatvaṃ na hi kvacit \|\| 13 \|\|*

12-13. In the Sakala aspect the worship of his embodied form is appropriate. Since He has the Sakala and Niṣkala aspects He is worshipped both in the phallic and in the embodied form by the people and is called the highest Brahman. Other deities, not being Brahman, have no Niṣkala aspect anywhere.

## \|\| 1.5.14 \|\|

*tasmātte niṣkale liṃge nārādhyaṃte sureśvarāḥ \|*  
*abrahmatvācca jīvatvāttathānye devatāgaṇāḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Hence the deities are not worshipped in the formless phallic symbol. The other deities are both non-Brahman and individual souls.

## \|\| 1.5.15 \|\|

*tūṣṇīṃ sakalamātratvādarcyaṃte beramātrake \|*  
*jīvatvaṃ śaṃkarānyeṣāṃ brahmatvaṃ śaṃkarasya ca \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. In view of their being embodied alone they are worshipped solely in the bodily form. Śaṅkara has Brahmatva and the others Jīvatva.

## \|\| 1.5.16 \|\|

*vedāṃtasārasaṃsiddhaṃ praṇavārthe prakāśanāt \|*  
*evameva purā pṛṣṭo maṃdare naṃdikeśvaraḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. This has been explained in the meaning of the Praṇava (Om), the essence of Vedānta, by Nandikeśvara when asked by Sanatkumāra, the intelligent son of Brahmā, at the mountain Mandara.

## \|\| 1.5.17-18 \|\|

*sanatkumāramuninā brahmaputreṇa dhīmatā \|*  
*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*śivānyadevavaśyānāṃ sarveṣāmapi sarvataḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

*beramātraṃ ca pūjārthaṃ śrutaṃ dṛṣṭaṃ ca bhūriśaḥ \|*  
*śivamātrasya pūjāyāṃ liṃgaṃ beraṃ ca dṛśyate \|\| 18 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

17-18. The embodied form alone is often observed in the worship of the deities other than Śiva. But both the phallic and the embodied forms are seen only in the worship of Śiva. Hence O benevolent one, please tell me precisely making me understand the truth.

## \|\| 1.5.19 \|\|

*atastadbrūhi kalyāṇa tattvaṃ me sādhubodhanam \|*  
*naṃdikeśvara uvāca \|*  
*anuttaramimaṃ praśnaṃ rahasyaṃ brahmalakṣaṇam \|\| 19 \|\|*

Nandikeśvara said:—

19\. It is impossible to answer this question without revealing the secret of Brahman.

## \|\| 1.5.20-24 \|\|

*kathayāmi śivenoktaṃ bhaktiyuktasya te'nagha \|*  
*śivasya brahmarūpatvānniṣkalatvācca niṣkalam \|\| 20 \|\|*

*liṃgaṃ tasyaiva pūjāyāṃ sarvavedeṣu saṃmatam \|*  
*tasyaiva sakalatvācca tathā sakalaniṣkalam \|\| 21 \|\|*

*sakalaṃ ca tathā beraṃ pūjāyāṃ lokasaṃmatam \|*  
*śivānyeṣāṃ ca jīvatvātsakalatvācca sarvataḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

*beramātraṃ ca pūjāyāṃ saṃmataṃ vedanirṇaye \|*  
*svāvirbhāve ca devānāṃ sakalaṃ rūpameva hi \|\| 23 \|\|*

*śivasya liṃgaṃ beraṃ ca darśane dṛśyate khalu \|*  
*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*uktaṃ tvayā mahābhāga liṃgaberapracāraṇam \|\| 24 \|\|*

20-24. O sinless one, since you are pious I shall tell you what Śiva Himself has said. Since Śiva has the bodiless aspect in virtue of His being the supreme Brahman, the Niṣkala liṅga, in conformity with the Vedic implication, is used only in His worship. Since He has an embodied form as well, His embodied form is also worshipped and accepted by all people. According to the decision in the Vedas, the embodied form alone is to be used in the worship of other deities who are only individual souls embodied. Devas have only the embodied aspect in their manifestation. In sacred literature both the phallic and the embodied forms are mentioned for Śiva.

## \|\| 1.5.25 \|\|

*śivasya ca tadanyeṣāṃ vibhajya paramārthataḥ \|*  
*tasmāttadeva paramaṃ liṃgaberādisaṃbhavam \|\| 25 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

25\. O Fortunate one, you have explained the worship of phallus and image distinctly for Śiva and the other deities. Hence, O lord of Yogins, I wish to hear the feature of the manifestation of the phallic aspect of Śiva.

## \|\| 1.5.26-27 \|\|

*śrotumicchāmi yogīṃdra liṃgāvirbhāvalakṣaṇam \|*  
*naṃdikeśvara uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇu vatsa bhavatprītyā vakṣyāmi paramārthataḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

*purā kalpe mahākāle prapanne lokaviśrute \|*  
*āyudhyetāṃ mahātmānau brahmaviṣṇū parasparam \|\| 27 \|\|*

Nandikeśvara said:—

26-27. O dear one, out of love for you I shall tell you the truth. Long long ago, in the famous first Kalpa, the noble souls Brahmā and Viṣṇu fought each other.

## \|\| 1.5.28 \|\|

*tayormānaṃ nirākartuṃ tanmadhye parameśvaraḥ \|*  
*niṣkalastaṃbharūpeṇa svarūpaṃ samadarśayat \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. In order to eradicate their arrogance lord Parameśvara showed his unembodied Niṣkala form in the form of a column in their midst.

## \|\| 1.5.29 \|\|

*tataḥ svaliṃgacihnatvātstaṃbhato niṣkalaṃ śivaḥ \|*  
*svaliṃgaṃ darśayāmāsa jagatāṃ hitakāmyayā \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. He showed his phallus emblem separate, evolved out of the column, with a desire to bless the worlds.

## \|\| 1.5.30 \|\|

*tadāprabhṛti lokeṣu niṣkalaṃ liṃgamaiśvaram \|*  
*sakalaṃ ca tathā beraṃ śivasyaiva prakalpitam \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. From that time onwards the divine phallus and the embodied image, both, were assigned to Śiva alone.

## \|\| 1.5.31 \|\|

*śivānyeṣaḥ tu devānāṃ beramātraṃ prakalpitam \|*  
*tattadberaṃ tu devānāṃ tattadbhogapradaṃ śubham \|*  
*śivasya liṃgaberatvaṃ bhogamokṣapradaṃ śubham \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. The embodied form alone was assigned to deities other than Śiva. The different types of the embodied forms of the different Devas yield only enjoyments. In regard to Śiva the phallic emblem and the embodied form together bestow auspicious enjoyment and salvation.

## Chapter 6 - Battle between Brahmā and Viṣṇu

## \|\| 1.6.1 \|\|

*naṃdikeśvara uvāca \|*  
*purā kadācidyogīṃdra viṣṇurviṣadharāsanaḥ \|*  
*suṣvāpa parayā bhūtyā svānugairapi saṃvṛtaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Summary: The journey to Kailāśa of the Devas terrified by the use of the Pāśupata weapon in the fight between Brahmā and Viṣṇu who vied with each other maintaining that each of them is the Lord himself.

Nandikeśvara said:—

1\. Once, long ago, O foremost among Yogins, Viṣṇu was having his nap on his serpent-couch. He was surrounded by the goddess of fortune and his attendants.

## \|\| 1.6.2 \|\|

*yadṛcchayā gatastatra brahmā brahmavidāṃvaraḥ \|*  
*apṛcchatpuṃḍarīkākṣaṃ śayanaṃ sarvasundaram \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. Brahmā, the foremost among the Vedic scholars chanced to come there. He asked the lotus-eyed handsome Viṣṇu who was lying there.

## \|\| 1.6.3 \|\|

*kastvaṃ puruṣavaccheṣe dṛṣṭvā māmapi dṛptavat \|*  
*uttiṣṭha vatsa māṃ paśya tava nāthamihāgatam \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Who are you lying here like a haughty person even after seeing me? Get up, O dear, and see me who am your lord. I have come here.

## \|\| 1.6.4 \|\|

*āgataṃ gurumārādhyaṃ dṛṣṭvā yo dṛptavaccaret \|*  
*dro hiṇastasya mūḍhasya prāyaścittaṃ vidhīyate \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Expiatory rites are ordained for that spiteful wretch who behaves like a haughty fool at the visit of an honourable elderly person.

## \|\| 1.6.5 \|\|

*iti śrutvā vacaḥ kruddho bahiḥ śāṃtavadācarat \|*  
*svasti te svāgataṃ vatsa tiṣṭha pīṭhamito viśa \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. On hearing these words Viṣṇu was angry. But assuming a calm exterior he said—“O dear, Hail thee. Welcome. Please sit on this couch. How is it that thy face is agitated and thy eyes look curious?

## \|\| 1.6.6 \|\|

*kimu te vyāgravadvaktraṃ vibhāti viṣamekṣaṇam \|*  
*brahmovāca \|*  
*vatsa viṣṇo mahāmānamāgataṃ kālavegataḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Brahmā said:—

6\. Dear Viṣṇu, know me to have come with the speed of the Time. I am to be honoured greatly. O dear one, I the protector of the world, Grandfather, your protector as well.

## \|\| 1.6.7 \|\|

*pitāmahaśca jagataḥ pātā ca tava vatsaka \|*  
*viṣṇuruvāca \|*  
*matsthaṃ jagadidaṃ vatsa manuṣe tvaṃ hi coravat \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. O dear one, the whole universe is situated within me but your way of thinking is like that of a thief. You are born of the lotus sprung from my navel-region. You are my son. Your words are futile therefore.

## \|\| 1.6.8-9 \|\|

*mannābhikamalājjātaḥ putrastvaṃ bhāṣase vṛthā \|*  
*naṃdikeśvara uvāca \|*  
*evaṃ hi vadatostatra mugdhayorajayostadā \|\| 8 \|\|*

*ahameva baro na tvamahaṃ prabhurahaṃ prabhuḥ \|*  
*parasparaṃ haṃtukāmau cakratuḥ samarodyamam \|\| 9 \|\|*

Nandikeśvara said:—

8-9. Arguing with each other like this, saying that each is better than the other and claiming to be the lord, they got ready to fight, like two foolish goats, desirous of killing each other.

## \|\| 1.6.10 \|\|

*yuyudhāte'marau vīrau haṃsapakṣīṃdra vāhanau \|*  
*vairaṃcyā vaiṣṇavāścaivaṃ mitho yuyudhire tadā \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. The two heroic deities, seated on their respective vehicles—the Swan and the Garuḍa, fought together. The attendants of Brahmā and Viṣṇu also came into clash.

## \|\| 1.6.11 \|\|

*tāvadvimānagatayaḥ sarvā vai devajātayaḥ \|*  
*didṛkṣavaḥ samājagmuḥ samaraṃ taṃ mahādbhutam \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. In the meantime the different groups of Devas moving about in aerial chariots came there to witness the wonderful fight.

## \|\| 1.6.12-18 \|\|

*kṣipaṃtaḥ puṣpavarṣāṇi paśyaṃtaḥ svairamaṃbare \|*  
*suparṇavāhanastatra kruddho vai brahmavakṣasi \|\| 12 \|\|*

*mumoca bāṇānasahānastrāṃśca vividhānbahūn \|*  
*mumocā'tha vidhiḥ kruddho viṣṇorurasi duḥsahān \|\| 13 \|\|*

*bāṇānanalasaṃkāśānastrāṃśca bahuśastadā \|*  
*tadāścaryamiti spaṣṭaṃ tayoḥ samaragocaram \|\| 14 \|\|*

*samīkṣya daivatagaṇāḥ śaśaṃsurbhṛśamākulāḥ \|*  
*tato viṣṇuḥ susaṃkruddhaḥ śvasanvyasanakarśitaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

*māheśvarāstraṃ matimān saṃdadhe brahmaṇopari \|*  
*tato brahmā bhṛśaṃ kruddhaḥ kaṃpayanviśvameva hi \|\| 16 \|\|*

*astraṃ pāśupataṃ ghoraṃ saṃdadhe viṣṇuvakṣasi \|*  
*tatastadutthitaṃ vyomni tapanāyutasannibham \|\| 17 \|\|*

*sahasramukhamatyugraṃ caṃḍavātabhayaṃkaram \|*  
*astradvayamidaṃ tatra brahmaviṣṇvorbhayaṃkaram \|\| 18 \|\|*

12-18. Witnessing from the heaven they scattered flowers everywhere. The Garuḍa-vehicled deity (Viṣṇu) became infuriated and discharged unbearable arrows and many kinds of weapons on the chest of Brahmā. The infuriated Brahmā also hurled many arrows of fiery fury and different kinds of weapons on Viṣṇu. The Devas commented on this wondrous fight and were agitated much, Viṣṇu in his great fury and mental agitation breathed hard and discharged the Māheśvara weapon over Brahmā. Annoyed at this, Brahmā aimed the terrible Pāśupata weapon at the chest of Viṣṇu. The weapon rising high in the sky blazing like ten thousand suns, with thousands of terrible pointed spikes roared awfully like a gust of wind. These two weapons of Brahmā and Viṣṇu thus faced each other in a terrible clash.

## \|\| 1.6.19 \|\|

*itthaṃ babhūva samaro brahmaviṣṇvoḥ parasparam \|*  
*tato devagaṇāḥ sarve viṣaṇṇā bhṛśamākulāḥ \|*  
*ūcuḥ parasparaṃ tāta rājakṣobhe yathā dvijāḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Such was the mutual fight between Brahmā and Viṣṇu. Then, O dear, the devas in their helpless agitation and vexation talked among themselves as people do at the time of war between their monarchs.

## \|\| 1.6.20-22 \|\|

*sṛṣṭiḥ sthitiśca saṃhārastiro bhāvopyanugrahaḥ \|*  
*yasmātpravartate tasmai brahmaṇe ca triśūline \|\| 20 \|\|*

*aśakyamanyairyadanugrahaṃ vinā tṛṇakṣayopyatra yadṛcchayā kvacit \|*  
*iti devābhayaṃ kṛtvā vicinvaṃtaḥ śivakṣayam \|\| 21 \|\|*

*jagmuḥ kailāsaśikharaṃ yatrāste caṃdra śekharaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

20-22. The three-pointed-trident-bearing deity, the supreme Brahman, (i.e. Śiva) is the cause of creation, maintenance, annihilation, concealment and blessing. Without His corroboration even a blade of grass cannot be split by any individual anywhere. Thinking thus in their fright they desired to go to Śiva’s abode and accordingly came to the summit of Kailāsa where the moon-crested God resided.

## \|\| 1.6.23 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvaivamamarā hṛṣṭāḥ padaṃtatpārameśvaram \|*  
*praṇemuḥ praṇavākāraṃ praviṣṭāstatra sadmani \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. On seeing that region of Parameśvara in the shape of Oṃkāra they bent their heads down in reverence and entered the palace.

## \|\| 1.6.24 \|\|

*tepi tatra sabhāmadhye maṃḍape maṇiviṣṭare \|*  
*virājamānamumayā dadṛśurdevapuṃgavam \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. There they saw the supreme leader of the Devas brilliantly shining on the gem-set seat in the company of Umā on an altar in the middle of the council-chamber.

## \|\| 1.6.25 \|\|

*savyottaretarapadaṃ tadarhitakarāṃ bujam \|*  
*svagaṇaiḥ sarvato juṣṭaṃ sarvalakṣaṇalakṣitam \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. His right leg was kept over the knee of the left; his lotus-like hands were placed over the legs; his attendants were all round him. He had all good characteristic features.

## \|\| 1.6.26 \|\|

*vījyamānaṃ viśeṣajaiḥ strījanaistīvrabhāvanaiḥ \|*  
*śasyamānaṃ sadāvedairanugṛhṇaṃtamīśvaram \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. He was being fanned by the specialists in that art—ladies of pointed attention. The Vedas were extolling Him. The lord was blessing every one.

## \|\| 1.6.27 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvaivamīśamamarāḥ saṃtoṣasalilekṣaṇāḥ \|*  
*daṃḍavaddūrato vatsa namaścakrurmahāgaṇāḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. On seeing the lord thus, the Devas shed tears of joy. O dear one, the hosts of Devas knelt down even from a great distance.

## \|\| 1.6.28 \|\|

*tānavekṣya patirdevānsamīpe cāhvayadgaṇaiḥ \|*  
*atha saṃhlādayandevāndevo devaśikhāmaṇiḥ \|*  
*avocadarthagaṃbhīraṃ vacanaṃ madhumaṃgalam \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. The lord, on seeing the Devas, beckoned them to him through his attendants. Then causing the delight of the Devas, the crest-jewel of Devas (i.e. Śiva), addressed them gravely with sweet auspicious words.

## Chapter 7 - Śiva manifests himself as a column of fire in the battlefield

## \|\| 1.7.1 \|\|

*īśvara uvāca \|*  
*vatsakāḥ svastivaḥ kaccidvartate mama śāsanāt \|*  
*jagacca devatāvaṃśaḥ svasvakarmaṇi kiṃ navā \|\| 1 \|\|*

Īśvara said:—

1\. Dear children, hail to ye. I hope the universe and the race of the deities, under my suzerainty, flourish in their respective duties.

## \|\| 1.7.2 \|\|

*prāgeva viditaṃ yuddhaṃ brahmaviṣṇvormayāsurāḥ \|*  
*bhavatāmabhitāpena paunaruktyena bhāṣitam \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. O gods, the fight between Brahmā and Viṣṇu is

already known to me. This agitation on your part is like a redundant speech.

## \|\| 1.7.3 \|\|

*iti sasmitayā mādhvyā kumāraparibhāṣayā \|*  
*samatoṣayadaṃbāyāḥ sa patistatsuravrajam \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Thus the consort of Ambā consoled the concourse of devas with honeylike speech sweetened with a smile in the manner of appeasing children.

## \|\| 1.7.4 \|\|

*atha yuddhāṃgaṇaṃ gaṃtuṃ haridhātroradhīśvaraḥ \|*  
*ājñāpayadgaṇeśānāṃ śataṃ tatraiva saṃsadi \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. In that very assembly the lord announced his desire to go to the battlefield of Hari and Brahmā and accordingly issued His directive to a hundred of the commanders of his attendants.

## \|\| 1.7.5-6 \|\|

*tato vādyaṃ bahuvidhaṃ prayāṇāya pareśituḥ \|*  
*gaṇeśvarāśca saṃnaddhā nānāvāhanabhūṣaṇāḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

*praṇavākāramādyaṃtaṃ paṃcamaṃḍalamaṃḍitam \|*  
*āruroha rathaṃ bhadra maṃbikāpatirīśvaraḥ \|*  
*sasūnugaṇamiṃdrā dyāḥ sarvepyanuyayuḥ surāḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

5-6. Different kinds of musical instruments were played to announce the start of the journey of the Lord. The commanders of the attendants were in readiness fully bedecked in their ornaments, seated in their respective vehicles. The lord, consort of Ambikā, mounted the holy chariot shaped like Oṃkāra from front to the back and embellished m five circular rings. He was accompanied by his sons and Gaṇas. All the devas, Indra and others, followed.

## \|\| 1.7.7 \|\|

*citradhvajavyajanacāmarapuṣpavarṣasaṃgatinṛtyanivahairapi vādyavargaiḥ \|*  
*saṃmānitaḥ paśupatiḥ parayā ca devyā sākaṃ tayoḥ samarabhūmimagātsasainyaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Honoured suitably by the display of banners of various colours, fans, chowries, scattered flowers, music, dance and the instrument players, and accompanied by the great goddess (Pārvatī), Paśupati (Śiva) went to the battle-field with the whole army.

## \|\| 1.7.8 \|\|

*samīkṣyaṃ tu tayoryuddhaṃ nigūḍho'bhraṃ samāsthitaḥ \|*  
*samāptavādyanirghoṣaḥ śāṃtorugaṇaniḥsvanaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. On espying the battle, the lord vanished in the firmament. The play of the music stopped and the tumult of the Gaṇas subsided.

## \|\| 1.7.9 \|\|

*atha brahmācyutau vīrau haṃtukāmau parasparam \|*  
*māheśvareṇa cā'streṇa tathā pāśupatena ca \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. There in the battlefield Brahmā and Acyuta desirous of killing each other were awaiting the result of the Māheś-vara and the Paśupata weapons hurled by them.

## \|\| 1.7.10-11 \|\|

*astrajvālairatho dagdhaṃ brahmaviṣṇvorjagattrayam \|*  
*īśopi taṃ nirīkṣyātha hyakālapralayaṃ bhṛśam \|\| 10 \|\|*

*mahānalastaṃbhavibhīṣaṇākṛtirbabhūva tanmadhyatale sa niṣkalaḥ \|*  
*te astre cāpi sajvāle lokasaṃharaṇakṣame \|\| 11 \|\|*

10-11. The flames emitted by the two weapons of Brahmā and Viṣṇu burned the three worlds. On seeing this imminent untimely dissolution the bodiless form of Śiva assumed the terrific form of a huge column of fire in their midst.

## \|\| 1.7.12 \|\|

*nipatetuḥ kṣaṇe naiva hyāvirbhūte mahānale \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. The two weapons of fiery flame potential enough to destroy the entire world fell into the huge column of fire that manifested itself there instantaneously.

## \|\| 1.7.13 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā tadadbhutaṃ citramastraśāṃtikaraṃ śubham \|*  
*kimetadadbhutākāramityūcuśca parasparam \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Seeing that auspicious wonderful phenomenon assuaging the weapons they asked each other “What is this wonderful form?”

## \|\| 1.7.14 \|\|

*atīṃdri yamidaṃ staṃbhamagnirūpaṃ kimutthitam \|*  
*asyordhvamapi cādhaśca āvayorlakṣyameva hi \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. “What is this column of fire that has risen up? It is beyond the range of senses. We have to find out its top and bottom.”

## \|\| 1.7.15 \|\|

*iti vyavasitau vīrau militau vīramāninau \|*  
*tatparau tatparīkṣārthaṃ pratasthāte'tha satvaram \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Jointly deciding like this, the two heroes proud of their prowess immediately set about assiduously in their quest.

## \|\| 1.7.16-18 \|\|

*āvayormiśrayostatra kāryamekaṃ na saṃbhavet \|*  
*ityuktvā sūkaratanurviṣṇustasyādimīyivān \|\| 16 \|\|*

*tathā brahmāhaṃ satanustadaṃtaṃ vīkṣituṃ yayau \|*  
*bhittvā pātālanilayaṃ gatvā dūrataraṃ hariḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

*nā'pśyāttasya saṃsthānaṃ staṃbhasyānalavarcasaḥ \|*  
*śrāṃtaḥ sa sūkarahariḥ prāpa pūrvaṃ raṇāṃgaṇam \|\| 18 \|\|*

16-18. “Nothing will turn up if we are together”. Saying this, Viṣṇu assumed the form of a Boar and went in search of the root. Brahmā in the form of a swan went up in search of the top. Piercing through the netherworlds and going very far below, Viṣṇu could not see the root of the fiery column. Utterly exhausted, Viṣṇu in the form of a Boar returned to the former battle-ground.

## \|\| 1.7.19 \|\|

*atha gacchaṃstu vyomnā ca vidhistāta pitā tava \|*  
*dadarśa ketakī puṣpaṃ kiṃcidvicyutamadbhutam \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Dear one, your father, Brahmā who went high up in the sky saw a certain bunch of Ketakī flower of mysterious nature falling from above.

## \|\| 1.7.20-21 \|\|

*atisaurabhyamamlānaṃ bahuvarṣacyutaṃ tathā \|*  
*anvīkṣya ca tayoḥ kṛtyaṃ bhagavānparameśvaraḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

*parihāsaṃ tu kṛtavānkaṃpanāccalitaṃ śiraḥ \|*  
*tasmāttāvanugṛhṇātuṃ cyutaṃ ketakamuttamam \|\| 21 \|\|*

20-21. On seeing the mutual fight of Brahmā and Viṣṇu, lord Śiva laughed. When his head shook, the Ketakī flower dropped down. Although it had been in its downward course for many years, neither its fragrance nor its lustre had been diminished even a bit. The flower had been intended to bless them.

## \|\| 1.7.22-23 \|\|

*kiṃ tvaṃ patasi puṣpeśa puṣparāṭ kena vā dhṛtam \|*  
*ādimasyāprameyasya staṃbhamadhyāccyutaściram \|\| 22 \|\|*

*na saṃpaśyāmi tasmāttvaṃ jahyāśāmaṃtadarśane \|*  
*asyāṃ tasya ca sevārthaṃ haṃsamūrtirihāgataḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

22-23. (Brahmā said) “O lord of flowers, by whom had you been worn? Why do you fall? I have come here to seek out the top, in the form of a swan.” (The flower replied) “I am falling down from the middle of this primordial column that is inscrutable. It has taken me a long time. Hence I do not see how you can see the top.”

## \|\| 1.7.24-25 \|\|

*itaḥ paraṃ sakhe me'dya tvayā kartavyamīpsitam \|*  
*mayā saha tvayā vācyametadviṣṇośca sannidhau \|\| 24 \|\|*

*staṃbhāṃto vīkṣito dhātrā tatra sākṣyahamacyuta \|*  
*ityuktvā ketakaṃ tatra praṇanāma punaḥ naḥ \|*  
*asatyamapi śastaṃ syādāpadītyanuśāsanam \|\| 25 \|\|*

24-25. “Dear friend, hereafter you must do as I desire. In the presence of Viṣṇu you must say like this. O Acyuta, the top of the column has been seen by Brahmā. I am the witness for the same.” Saying this he bowed to the Ketakī flower again and again. Even falsehood is recommended in times of danger. So say the authoritative texts.

## \|\| 1.7.26 \|\|

*samīkṣya tatrā'cyutamāyataśramaṃ pranaṣṭaharṣaṃ tu nanarta harṣāt \|*  
*uvāca cainaṃ paramārthamacyutaṃ ṣaṃḍhāttavādaḥ sa vidhistato'cyutam \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. (Returning to the original place) on seeing Viṣṇu there, utterly exhausted and lacking pleasure, Brahmā danced with joy. Viṣṇu, in the manner of a eunuch admitting his inability(to a woman), told him the truth (that he could not see the bottom). But Brahmā told Viṣṇu like this.

## \|\| 1.7.27-28 \|\|

*staṃbhāgrametatsamudīkṣitaṃ hare tatraiva sākṣī nanu ketakaṃ tvidam \|*  
*tato'vadattatra hi ketakaṃ mṛṣā tatheti taddhātṛvacastadaṃtike \|\| 27 \|\|*

*hariśca tatsatyamitīva ciṃtayaṃścakāra tasmai vidhaye namaḥ svayam \|*  
*ṣoḍaśairupacāraiśca pūjayāmāsa taṃ vidhim \|\| 28 \|\|*

27-28. “O Hari, the top of this column has been seen by me. This Ketakī flower is my witness.” The Ketaka flower repeated the falsehood endorsing the words of Brahmā in his presence. Hari, taking it to be true, made obeisance to Brahmā. He worshipped Brahmā with all the sixteen means of service and homage.

## \|\| 1.7.29 \|\|

*vidhiṃ prahartuṃ śaṭhamagniliṃgataḥ sa īśvarastatra babhūva sākṛtiḥ \|*  
*samutthitaḥ svāmi vilokanātpunaḥ prakaṃpapāṇiḥ parigṛhya tatpadam \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. The Lord taking up a visible form in order to chastise Brahmā who practised trickery, came out of the column of fire. On seeing the lord, Viṣṇu stood up and with his hands shaking with fear caught hold of the lord’s feet.

## \|\| 1.7.30 \|\|

*ādyaṃtahīnavapuṣi tvayi mohabuddhyā bhūyādvimarśa iha nāvati kāmanotthaḥ \|*  
*sa tvaṃ prasīda karuṇākara kaśmalaṃ nau mṛṣṭaṃ kṣamasva vihitaṃ bhavataiva kelyā \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. It is out of ignorance and delusion about you whose body is without a beginning or an end that we indulged in this quest prompted by our own desire. Hence O, Sympathetic Being, forgive us for our fault. In fact, it is but another form of your divine sport.

## \|\| 1.7.31 \|\|

*īśvara uvāca \|*  
*vatsaprasanno'smi hare yatastvamīśatvamicchannapi satyavākyam \|*  
*brūyāstataste bhavitā janeṣu sāmyaṃ mayā satkṛtirapyalapthāḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

Īśvara said:—

31\. “O dear Hari, I am pleased with you, because you strictly adhered to truth in spite of your desire to be a lord. Hence among the general public you will have a footing equal to mine. You will be honoured too likewise.

## \|\| 1.7.32 \|\|

*itaḥ paraṃ te pṛthagātmanaśca kṣetrapratiṣṭhotsavapūjanaṃ ca \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Hereafter you will be separate from me having separate temple, installation of idols, festivals and worship.”

## \|\| 1.7.33 \|\|

*iti devaḥ purā prītaḥ satyena haraye param \|*  
*dadau svasāmyamatyarthaṃ devasaṃghe ca paśyati \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Thus, formerly, the lord was delighted by the truthfulness of Hari and offered him a footing equal to his own even as the assembly of the devas was witnessing the same.

## Chapter 8 - Śiva’s forgiveness of Brahmā

## \|\| 1.8.1 \|\|

*naṃdikeśvara uvāca \|*  
*sasarjātha mahādevaḥ puruṣaṃ kaṃcidadbhutam \|*  
*bhairavākhyaṃ bhruvormadhyādbrahmadarpajighāṃsayā \|\| 1 \|\|*

Nandikeśvara said:—

1\. Mahādeva then created a wonderful person, Bhairava, from the middle of his brows to quell the pride of Brahmā.

## \|\| 1.8.2 \|\|

*sa vai tadā tatra patiṃ praṇamya śivamaṃgaṇe \|*  
*kiṃ kāryaṃ karavāṇyatra śīghramājñāpaya prabho \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. This Bhairava knelt before the lord in the battle-field and said—“O lord, what shall I do? Please give me your directives quickly.”

## \|\| 1.8.3 \|\|

*vatsayo'yaṃ vidhiḥ sākṣājjagatāmādyadaivatam \|*  
*nūnamarcaya khaḍgena tigmena javasā param \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. “Dear, here is Brahmā, the first deity of the universe. Worship him with your sharp-pointed quick-moving sword.”

## \|\| 1.8.4 \|\|

*sa vai gṛhītvaikakareṇa keśaṃ tatpaṃcamaṃ dṛptamasatyabhāṣaṇam \|*  
*chittvā śirāṃsyasya nihaṃtumudyataḥ prakaṃpayankhaḍgamatisphuṭaṃ karaiḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. With one of his hands he caught hold of the tuft of Brahma’s fifth head that was guilty of haughtily uttering a falsehood, and with the hands he furiously shook his sword in order to cut it off.

## \|\| 1.8.5 \|\|

*pitā tavotsṛṣṭavibhūṣaṇāṃbarasraguttarīyāmalakeśasaṃhatiḥ \|*  
*pravātaraṃbheva lateva caṃcalaḥ papāta vai bhairavapādapaṃkaje \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Your father trembled like a plantain tree in a whirlwind, with his ornaments scattered here and there, his cloth ruffled and loosened, the garland displaced, the upper cloth hanging loose and the glossy tuft dishevelled, and fell at the feet of Bhairava.

## \|\| 1.8.6 \|\|

*tāvadvidhiṃ tāta didṛkṣuracyutaḥ kṛpālurasmatpatipādapallavam \|*  
*niṣicya bāṣpairavadatkṛtāṃjaliryathā śiśuḥ svaṃ pitaraṃ kalākṣaram \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Meanwhile the sympathetic Acyuta desirous of saving Brahmā, shed tears over the lotus-like feet of our lord and said with palms joined in reverence just like a child lisping words of entreaty to its father.

## \|\| 1.8.7 \|\|

*acyuta uvāca \|*  
*tvayā prayatnena purā hi dattaṃ yadasya paṃcānanamīśacihnam \|*  
*tasmātkṣamasvādyamanugrahārhaṃ kuru prasādaṃ vidhaye hyamuṣmai \|\| 7 \|\|*

Acyuta said:—

7\. O Lord, it was you who gave him five heads as a special symbol, long ago. Hence please forgive him his first guilt. Please favour him.

## \|\| 1.8.8 \|\|

*ityarthito'cyuteneśastuṣṭaḥ suragaṇāṃgaṇe \|*  
*nivartayāmāsa tadā bhairavaṃ brahmadaṃḍataḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. The lord thus requested by Acyuta relented and in the presence of all devas asked Bhairava to desist from punishing Brahmā.

## \|\| 1.8.9 \|\|

*athāha devaḥ kitavaṃ vidhiṃ vigatakaṃdharam \|*  
*brahmaṃstvamarhaṇākāṃkṣī śaṭhamīśatvamāsthitaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Then the lord turned to the deceitful Brahmā who bent down his neck and said “O Brahmā, in order to extort honour from the people you assumed the role of the lord in a roguish manner.

## \|\| 1.8.10-11 \|\|

*nātaste satkṛtirloke bhūyātsthānotsavādikam \|*  
*brahmovaca \|*  
*svāminprasīdādya mahāvibhūte manye varaṃ varada me śirasaḥ pramokṣam \|\| 10 \|\|*

*namastubhyaṃ bhagavate baṃdhave viśvayonaye \|*  
*sahiṣṇave sarvadoṣāṇāṃ śaṃbhave śailadhanvane \|\| 11 \|\|*

10-11. Hence you shall not be honoured, nor shall you have your own temple or festival.

## \|\| 1.8.12 \|\|

*īśvara uvāca \|*  
*arājabhayametadvai jagatsarvaṃ na śiṣyati \|*  
*tatastvaṃ jahi daṃḍārhaṃ vaha lokadhuraṃ śiśo \|\| 12 \|\|*

Brahmā said:—

O Lord, be pleased. O flourishing one, I consider this sparing of my head itself a great blessing and a boon. Obeisance to Thee, the lord, the kinsman, the originator of the universe, the forbearing, the forgiver of defects, the benevolent one, wielding the mountain as his bow.

Īśvara said:—

12\. O child, the whole universe will be ruined if it loses the fear of a king Hence you mete out punishment to the guilty and bear the burden of administering this universe.

## \|\| 1.8.13-14 \|\|

*varaṃ dadāmi te tatra gṛhāṇa durlabhaṃ param \|*  
*vaitānikeṣu gṛhyeṣu yajñe ca bhavān guruḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

*niṣphalastvadṛte yajñaḥ sāṃgaśca sahadakṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*athāha devaḥ kitavaṃ ketakaṃ kūṭasākṣiṇam \|\| 14 \|\|*

13-14. I shall grant you another boon which is very difficult to get. In all domestic and public sacrifices you will be the presiding deity. Even though a sacrifice is complete with all the ancillary rites and offerings of monetary gifts, it will be fruitless without you. Then the lord turned to the deceitful Ketaka flower guilty of perjury and said:—

## \|\| 1.8.15 \|\|

*re re ketaka duṣṭastvaṃ śaṭha dūramito vraja \|*  
*mamāpi prema te puṣpe mā bhūtpūjāsvitaḥ param \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. “O you Ketaka flower, you are roguish and deceitful. Go away from here. Hereafter I have no desire to include you in my worship.”

## \|\| 1.8.16 \|\|

*ityukte tatra devena ketakaṃ devajātayaḥ \|*  
*sarvāni vārayāmāsustatpārśvādanyatastadā \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. When the lord said thus, all the devas shunned the very presence of the flower.

## \|\| 1.8.17 \|\|

*ketaka uvāca \|*  
*namaste nātha me janmaniṣphalaṃ bhavadājñayā \|*  
*saphalaṃ kriyatāṃ tāta kṣamyatāṃ mama kilbiṣam \|\| 17 \|\|*

Ketaka said:—

17\. Obeisance to Thee, O Lord, Your bidding will mean that my very birth is fruitless. May the lord be pleased to make it fruitful by forgiving my sin.

## \|\| 1.8.18 \|\|

*jñānājñānakṛtaṃ pāpaṃ nāśayatyeva te smṛtiḥ \|*  
*tādṛśe tvayi dṛṣṭe me mithyādoṣaḥ kuto bhavet \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Thy remembrance is reputed to quell all sins perpetrated consciously or unconsciously. Now that I have seen Thee, how can the sin of uttering falsehood sully me?

## \|\| 1.8.19-21 \|\|

*tathā stutastu bhagavānketakena sabhātale \|*  
*na me tvaddhāraṇaṃ yogyaṃ satyavāgahamīśvaraḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

*madīyāstvāṃ dhariṣyaṃti janma te saphalaṃ tataḥ \|*  
*tvaṃ vai vitānavyājena mamopari bhaviṣyasi \|\| 20 \|\|*

*ityanugṛhya bhagavānketakaṃ vidhimādhavau \|*  
*virarāja sabhāmadhye sarvadevairabhiṣṭutaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

19-21. Thus entreated in the middle of the council the lord said—“It is not proper for me to wear you. I am the lord and my words must stay true. My attendants and followers shall wear thee. Hence thy birth shall be fruitful. Of course in the canopies over my idol you can be used for decoration.” The lord thus blessed the three—the flower Ketaka, Brahmā and Viṣṇu. He shone in the assembly duly eulogised by the Devas.

## Chapter 9 - The Proclamation of Śiva as Maheśvara (the great lord)

## \|\| 1.9.2 \|\|

*tatra saṃsthāpya tau devaṃ sakuṭuṃbaṃ varāsane \|*  
*pūjayāmāsatuḥ pūjyaṃ puṇyaiḥ puruṣavastubhiḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Nandikeśvara said:—

I. In the mean time Brahmā and Viṣṇu had been standing silently on either side of the lord with the palms joined in reverence.

2\. Then they installed the lord with all the members of His family on a splendid seat and worshipped Him with all holy personal things.

## \|\| 1.9.3-6 \|\|

*pauruṣaṃ prākṛtaṃ vastujñeyaṃ dīrghālpakālikam \|*
